  wallet at ₹0, 295 transactions all RECHARGE/CHAT_CONSUMPTION). Since no
  SIGNUP_BONUS reason ever existed, 100% of historical balance is provably
  purchased-origin — backfill + CHECK-constraint reconciliation strategy
  included.
  3. Ledger — redesigned as WalletTransaction + child WalletLedgerAllocation
  rows so one charge can split across both buckets; refunds mirror the original
  split exactly, never re-run the promotional-first policy.
  4. Rolling reservations — chat sessions now hold a rolling lookahead
  reservation (re-topped every tick) so concurrent tool executions and chat
  billing both see the same true "available" balance.
  5. Disconnect/downtime — introduced presence heartbeats, a new PAUSED state,
  and reconnect grace windows; backend restarts now
  pause-and-require-fresh-presence instead of billing the outage gap.
  6. Concurrency — switched to Postgres partial unique indexes
  (chat_sessions_one_active_per_user/astrologer), matching this repo's own
  existing precedent for exactly this pattern.
  7. Free-trial migration — reported the exact affected user count (1) and gave
  three options (A/B/C) for your sign-off rather than deciding silently.
  8. Tool billing — audited every AI route individually; found real risks (6x
  internal retries on /life-path, silent non-AI fallbacks reported as 200 OK, a
  shared endpoint with no feature identity) and designed settlement around
  actual AI usage per HTTP request, not a blanket wrapper.
  9. Mock payments — added a default-deny production guard requiring an
  explicit, documented env override.
  10. Pricing — carried forward the verified provider-pricing table with
  sources.

  Two AskUserQuestion decisions you already made are folded in (reuse-in-place
  wallet approach; new isComplimentary flag for free astrologers). Five smaller
  items remain open at the bottom of the plan (exact heartbeat/grace timings,
  reservation window, retry-UX fix, historical-trial option, tool prices) —
  still waiting for your approval before any coding begins.

✻ Brewed for 6m 33s

❯ /rename coin-based-astro 
  ⎿  Session renamed to: coin-based-astro

❯ MyBhagya Coin Wallet Architecture v2 is approved in principle.                
                                                                                
  I am finalizing the remaining business decisions and requesting a few         
  targeted technical corrections before implementation.                         
                                                                                
  ### Final business decisions                                                  
                                                                                
  1. Historical free-trial migration: Option A. Give all existing users the new 
  60-second global allowance. Preserve historical trial records.                
  2. Karma retry behavior: one user-confirmed action = one billable execution.  
  Internal automatic retries must share the same execution ID and never cause   
  another coin charge.                                                          
  3. Presence settings: heartbeat every 10 seconds, presence timeout 15         
  seconds, reconnect grace 60 seconds. Keep configurable in backend constants.  
  4. Chat reservation lookahead: 30 seconds, allowing smaller reservations when 
  the user has insufficient funds for the full window.                          
  5. Tool pricing: keep all new paid tool prices inactive until I explicitly    
  approve the price list.                                                       
  6. Complimentary astrologers: only Superadmin can enable isComplimentary.     
  Maximum complimentary session duration is initially 15 minutes, configurable  
  by Superadmin. These sessions deduct no coins and consume none of the global  
  60-second allowance.                                                          
                                                                                
  ### Required technical corrections                                            
                                                                                
  **1. Database session uniqueness**                                            
                                                                                
  Include REQUESTED, ACTIVE and PAUSED in both partial unique indexes for users 
  and astrologers.                                                              
                                                                                
  Persist and enforce request expiry, including after backend restarts.         
                                                                                
  **2. Wallet transaction concurrency**                                         
                                                                                
  Use database-level wallet row locking or equivalent atomic conditional        
  operations.                                                                   
                                                                                
  All balance calculations, bucket allocations, reservation changes and ledger  
  writes must happen atomically.                                                
                                                                                
  Add SQL CHECK constraints ensuring:                                           
  - purchasedBalancePaise >= 0                                                  
  - promotionalBalancePaise >= 0                                                
  - reservedBalancePaise >= 0                                                   
  - balancePaise = purchasedBalancePaise + promotionalBalancePaise              
  - reservedBalancePaise <= balancePaise                                        
                                                                                
  Prevent concurrent operations with different idempotency keys from            
  overspending the same wallet.                                                 
                                                                                
  **3. Rolling chat settlement**                                                
                                                                                
  Define the exact transaction order for consuming existing reservations,       
  settling billable seconds, and reserving future seconds.                      
                                                                                
  Never charge beyond available funds.                                          
                                                                                
  When funds are exhausted, calculate the exact affordable duration and end     
  billing at that boundary, including when exhaustion happens between billing   
  ticks.                                                                        
                                                                                
  **4. Server-authoritative chat timing**                                       
                                                                                
  Use socket connection state and server-validated heartbeats.                  
                                                                                
  Persist billable intervals or equivalent durable timing checkpoints.          
                                                                                
  Do not bill backend downtime, paused intervals, or time beyond the defined    
  presence cutoff.                                                              
                                                                                
  Do not equate an unfocused browser tab with a disconnected consultation.      
                                                                                
  Explicitly document that paid live chat is billed by consultation duration    
  rather than message count.                                                    
                                                                                
  **5. Production mock-payment security**                                       
                                                                                
  Remove ALLOW_MOCK_PAYMENTS_IN_PROD entirely.                                  
                                                                                
  Mock recharge must never credit coins when NODE_ENV=production.               
                                                                                
  Use a separate non-production QA environment for mock payments.               
                                                                                
  **6. Tool execution idempotency**                                             
                                                                                
  One user-confirmed action must have one durable execution ID.                 
                                                                                
  Internal provider retries must not create separate customer charges.          
                                                                                
  If the provider succeeds but the HTTP response is lost, the user must be able 
  to retrieve the original paid result without another charge.                  
                                                                                
  Use a durable reconciliation state for uncertain outcomes.                    
                                                                                
  **7. Signup bonus eligibility**                                               
                                                                                
  Grant the 50-coin bonus only to eligible newly activated accounts.            
                                                                                
  Ensure repeated OTP verification, Google account linking, and concurrent      
  requests cannot create duplicate grants.                                      
                                                                                
  **8. Migration correctness**                                                  
                                                                                
  Keep existing mock payment history identifiable as MOCK, not real-money       
  revenue.                                                                      
                                                                                
  Put database CHECK constraints in raw SQL migrations, not unsupported Prisma  
  @@check syntax.                                                               
                                                                                
  Verify generated migration SQL against the actual database schema and         
  migration history.                                                            
                                                                                
  ### Implementation instructions                                               
                                                                                
  Do not rewrite the entire architecture again.                                 
                                                                                
  Apply these targeted corrections to v2, then proceed with checkpoint 1        
  implementation after presenting the corrected migration and transaction       
  strategy.                                                                     
                                                                                
  Do not run database migrations without my explicit approval.                  
                                                                                
  Do not remove or break existing authentication, user roles, astrologer        
  onboarding, chat history, frontend pages, or current wallet features.         
                                                                                
  After each checkpoint, report changed files, tests, remaining risks, and next 
  steps.

      174 + * Reverses a DEBIT transaction, crediting back the EXACT orig

      183 +  const idempotencyKey = `refund:${originalTransactionId}`;

          +here: { idempotencyKey } });

  Checkpoint 1 complete: wallet buckets, split-bucket ledger, signup bonus

  Changed/added files:
  - backend/prisma/schema/payment.prisma — Wallet gains
  purchasedBalancePaise/promotionalBalancePaise/reservedBalancePaise;
  WalletTransaction gains idempotencyKey/reversalOfId; new
  WalletLedgerAllocation, SignupBonusGrant models; new WalletBalanceBucket enum;
  WalletTransactionReason extended with
  SIGNUP_BONUS/TOOL_EXECUTION/RESERVATION_RELEASE.
  - backend/prisma/schema/auth.prisma — added User.signupBonusGrant relation
  (additive).
  -
  backend/prisma/migrations/20261110140000_add_wallet_coin_buckets/migration.sql
  — hand-written, not applied. Verified by hand against a live introspection of
  the actual Neon dev DB (columns/enum labels/migration history) before writing
  a single statement. Includes the data backfill (100% of historical balance →
  PURCHASED, justified by the fact that SIGNUP_BONUS never existed before this
  migration) and all five CHECK constraints, added last so their automatic
  validation is the final proof the backfill was correct.
  - backend/src/modules/payment/wallet.service.ts — rewritten: lockWallet
  (SELECT ... FOR UPDATE), creditToBucket/debitWithAllocations/refundTransaction
  (locked, idempotent, split-bucket), plus the old credit/debit signatures kept
  as thin wrappers so payment.service.ts and chat/billing.ts keep working
  unchanged.
  - backend/src/modules/payment/signupBonus.service.ts — new.
  grantSignupBonusIfEligible(tx, userId) using INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (user_id) 
  DO NOTHING RETURNING id — never a try/catch around a unique-violation.
  - backend/src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts — wired into all three
  account-activation paths: verifyOtp (email), loginWithGoogleProfile (new-user
  branch only — the existing-user-linking branch is untouched), and verifyPhone
  (found during implementation as a third activation path your corrections
  didn't explicitly name — included for the same exactly-once guarantee).
  - backend/src/modules/payment/payment.service.ts — added
  assertMockPaymentsAllowed(), unconditional NODE_ENV === "production" block, no
  override flag.
  - backend/src/utils/statusCodes.ts — added SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE: 503.

● Don't proceed with that prompt — answer N (or Ctrl+C) to cancel it right now.

  Here's why: that's prisma db push, not prisma migrate deploy. db push does its
  own live diff against the schema files and applies only the structural
  changes (columns/tables/indexes) — it would skip entirely:
  - The data backfill (classifying existing balance as purchased, backfilling
  wallet_ledger_allocations for your 295 existing transactions)
  - All five CHECK constraints (they only exist in the hand-written migration
  SQL, never in schema.prisma, since Prisma can't express them declaratively)

  If you let it proceed, you'd end up with
  purchased_balance_paise/promotional_balance_paise sitting at 0 for every
  existing wallet even though they have a real balance — silently violating the
  exact invariant this migration exists to establish, with no error (because
  there's no CHECK constraint yet to catch it).

  The unique-constraint warning itself is harmless noise in this case (the
  column doesn't exist yet, so there can't be any duplicate values to conflict).

  What to run instead:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

  (20261110140000_add_wallet_coin_buckets) on top of your already-applied

  and tested them in Docker. It won't prompt you — if something's wrong it fails

      139 +enum WalletReservationPurpose {                               
      140 +  TOOL_EXECUTION                                              
      141 +  CHAT_LOOKAHEAD                                              
      142 +}                                                             
      143 +                                                              
      144 +enum WalletReservationStatus {                                
      145 +  HELD                                                        
      146 +  SETTLED                                                     
      147 +  RELEASED                                                    
      148 +  RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED                                     
      149 +}                                                             
      150 +                                                              
      151 +/// A database-backed hold against a wallet's balance — raises
          + Wallet.reservedBalancePaise so a                             
      152 +/// concurrent operation on the SAME wallet (another tool exec
          +ution, another chat session) can                              
      153 +/// never spend coins this one is already counting on. Reserva
          +tion creation never moves money                               
      154 +/// between buckets (that only happens at settlement, via the 
          +normal promotional-first debit                                
      155 +/// policy) — it only shrinks what `balancePaise - reservedBal
          +ancePaise` reports as truly available.                        
      156 +///                                                           
      157 +/// CHAT_LOOKAHEAD reservations are "rolling": created at sess
          +ion start for a short lookahead                               
      158 +/// window (chat.constants.ts's CHAT_RESERVATION_LOOKAHEAD_SEC
          +ONDS), partially settled and                                  
      159 +/// topped back up on every billing tick (see chat/billing.ts)
          +, and released for whatever's left                            
      160 +/// unused when the session ends. TOOL_EXECUTION reservations 
          +are one-shot: created once per                                
      161 +/// ToolExecution, settled or released exactly once.          
      162 +model WalletReservation {                                     
      163 +  id String @id @default(cuid())                              
      164 +                                                              
      165 +  walletId String @map("wallet_id")                           
      166 +  wallet   Wallet @relation(fields: [walletId], references: [i
          +d], onDelete: Cascade)                                        
      167 +                                                              
      168 +  amountPaise Int                      @map("amount_paise") //
          + currently-held amount; shrinks as it's settled, grows on top-
          +up                                                            
      169 +  purpose     WalletReservationPurpose                        
      170 +  /// ChatSession.id for CHAT_LOOKAHEAD, ToolExecution.executi
          +onId for TOOL_EXECUTION.                                      
      171 +  referenceId String                   @map("reference_id")   
      172 +  status      WalletReservationStatus  @default(HELD)         
      173 +                                                              
      174 +  createdAt  DateTime  @default(now()) @map("created_at")     
      175 +  resolvedAt DateTime? @map("resolved_at")                    
      176 +                                                              
      177 +  @@index([walletId, status])                                 
      178 +  @@index([referenceId])                                      
      179 +  @@map("wallet_reservations")                                
      180 +}                                                             
      181 +                                                              
      182  /// A recharge attempt. CREATED until the gateway (mock today,
            a real one later) confirms
      183  /// it; confirmation is the only thing allowed to credit the w
           allet, and confirming an
      184  /// already-SUCCESS order is a no-op so a retried/duplicate ca
           llback can never double-credit.

      274 +// Reservations — database-backed holds against Wallet.reserve
          +dBalancePaise. Creating a                                     
      275 +// reservation never moves money between buckets; it only shri

      276 +// `balancePaise - reservedBalancePaise` reports as truly avai

      280 +                                                              
      281  /**

          +ver is actually available rather

          +Paise` (which may be less than                                
      284 + * requested, or 0) and decides what a partial/zero reservatio
          +n means for its own flow (e.g. chat                           
      285 + * billing rejects session start on 0; a tool execution should
          + treat anything less than the full                            
      286 + * quoted price as insufficient balance).

      288 +export async function createReservation(                      
      289 +  walletId: string,                                           
      290 +  amountPaise: number,                                        
      291 +  purpose: WalletReservationPurpose,                          
      292 +  referenceId: string,                                        
      293 +  db: Db = prisma,                                            
      294 +): Promise<{ reservation: WalletReservation | null; reservedAm
          +ountPaise: number }> {                                        
      295 +  if (amountPaise <= 0) return { reservation: null, reservedAm
          +ountPaise: 0 };                                               
      296 +                                                              
      297 +  const run = async (tx: Prisma.TransactionClient) => {       
      298 +    const wallet = await lockWallet(tx, walletId);            
      299 +    const available = Math.max(0, wallet.balancePaise - wallet
          +.reservedBalancePaise);                                       
      300 +    const reserveAmount = Math.min(amountPaise, available);   
      301 +    if (reserveAmount <= 0) return { reservation: null, reserv
          +edAmountPaise: 0 };                                           
      302 +

          +mount;                                                        
      304 +    await tx.$executeRaw`UPDATE wallets SET reserved_balance_p
          +aise = ${newReserved}, updated_at = now() WHERE id = ${walletI
          +d}`;                                                          
      305 +    const reservation = await tx.walletReservation.create({ da
          +ta: { walletId, amountPaise: reserveAmount, purpose, reference
          +Id } });                                                      
      306 +    return { reservation, reservedAmountPaise: reserveAmount }
          +;                                                             
      307 +  };                                                          
      308 +                                                              
      309 +  return withTransaction(db, run);                            
      310 +}                                                             
      311 +                                                              
      312 +/**                                                           
      313 + * Tops a HELD reservation back up toward `targetAmountPaise`,
          + again degrading gracefully — adds                            
      314 + * as much as the wallet's current true availability allows, w
          +hich may be less than requested, or                           
      315 + * nothing. A no-op (returns the reservation unchanged) if it'

      318 +export async function topUpReservation(reservationId: string, 
          +targetAmountPaise: number, db: Db = prisma): Promise<WalletRes
          +ervation> {                                                   
      319 +  const run = async (tx: Prisma.TransactionClient) => {

          +rThrow({ where: { id: reservationId } });

      322 +

          +;                                                             
      325 +    if (needed <= 0) return reservation;

          +.reservedBalancePaise);

          +ionId }, data: { amountPaise: reservation.amountPaise + additi
          +onal } });                                                    
      334 +  };                                                          
      335 +                                                              
      336 +  return withTransaction(db, run);                            
      337 +}

      339 +/**                                                           
      340 + * Settles up to `amountPaise` against a HELD reservation: dra
          +ws from the reservation's currently                           
      341 + * held amount first, and if that's not enough, covers the sho
          +rtfall directly from the wallet's                             
      342 + * truly-available balance (never from coins another reservati
          +on is holding). Returns exactly how                           
      343 + * much was actually charged, which may be less than requested
          + — `exhausted: true` tells the                                
      344 + * caller funds ran out at this exact call, so it can stop bil
          +ling at the precise boundary instead                          
      345 + * of overshooting and correcting afterward. Idempotent via `i
          +dempotencyKey`.                                               
      346 + */                                                           
      347 +export async function settleFromReservation(                  
      348 +  reservationId: string,                                      
      349 +  amountPaise: number,                                        
      350 +  reason: WalletTransactionReason,                            
      351 +  opts: { referenceId?: string; idempotencyKey?: string } = {}
          +,                                                             
      352 +  db: Db = prisma,                                            
      353 +): Promise<{ wallet: Wallet; transaction: WalletTransaction | 
          +null; charged: number; exhausted: boolean }> {                
      354 +  const run = async (tx: Prisma.TransactionClient) => {       
      355 +    if (opts.idempotencyKey) {                                
      356 +      const existing = await tx.walletTransaction.findUnique({
          + where: { idempotencyKey: opts.idempotencyKey } });           
      357 +      if (existing) {                                         
      358 +        const wallet = await tx.wallet.findUniqueOrThrow({ whe
          +re: { id: existing.walletId } });                             
      359 +        return { wallet, transaction: existing, charged: exist
          +ing.amountPaise, exhausted: false };                          
      360 +      }                                                       
      361 +    }                                                         
      362 +                                                              
      363 +    const reservation = await tx.walletReservation.findUniqueO
          +rThrow({ where: { id: reservationId } });                     
      364 +    const wallet = await lockWallet(tx, reservation.walletId);
      365 +                                                              
      366 +    const fromReservation = Math.min(amountPaise, reservation.
          +amountPaise);                                                 
      367 +    const remainder = amountPaise - fromReservation;          
      368 +    // reservedBalancePaise already counts this reservation's 
          +current hold, so the balance beyond                           
      369 +    // it (not yet touched by this call) is what's left for an
          +y shortfall this reservation can't cover.                     
      370 +    const availableBeyondReservation = Math.max(0, wallet.bala
          +ncePaise - wallet.reservedBalancePaise);                      
      371 +    const fromWalletDirect = Math.min(remainder, availableBeyo
          +ndReservation);                                               
      372 +    const charged = fromReservation + fromWalletDirect;       
      373 +    const exhausted = charged < amountPaise;                  
      374 +                                                              
      375 +    if (charged <= 0) {                                       
      376 +      return { wallet, transaction: null, charged: 0, exhauste
          +d: true };                                                    
      377 +    }                                                         
      378 +                                                              
      379 +    const allocations = planDebitAllocations(wallet, charged);
      380 +    const newBalance = wallet.balancePaise - charged;         
      381 +    const newPromo = wallet.promotionalBalancePaise - sumBucke
          +t(allocations, "PROMOTIONAL");                                
      382 +    const newPurchased = wallet.purchasedBalancePaise - sumBuc
          +ket(allocations, "PURCHASED");                                
      383 +    const newReserved = wallet.reservedBalancePaise - fromRese
          +rvation;                                                      
      384 +                                                              
      385 +    await tx.$executeRaw`                                     
      386 +      UPDATE wallets                                          
      387 +      SET balance_paise = ${newBalance}, promotional_balance_p
          +aise = ${newPromo}, purchased_balance_paise = ${newPurchased},
      388 +          reserved_balance_paise = ${newReserved}, updated_at 
          += now()                                                       
      389 +      WHERE id = ${wallet.id}                                 
      390 +    `;                                                        
      391 +    await tx.walletReservation.update({ where: { id: reservati
          +onId }, data: { amountPaise: reservation.amountPaise - fromRes
          +ervation } });                                                
      392 +    const transaction = await tx.walletTransaction.create({   
      393 +      data: {                                                 
      394 +        walletId: wallet.id,                                  
      395 +        type: "DEBIT",                                        
      396 +        reason,                                               
      397 +        amountPaise: charged,                                 
      398 +        balanceAfterPaise: newBalance,                        
      399 +        referenceId: opts.referenceId,                        
      400 +        idempotencyKey: opts.idempotencyKey,                  
      401 +        allocations: { create: allocations },                 
      402 +      },                                                      
      403 +    });                                                       
      404 +    const updatedWallet = await tx.wallet.findUniqueOrThrow({ 
          +where: { id: wallet.id } });                                  
      405 +    return { wallet: updatedWallet, transaction, charged, exha
          +usted };                                                      
      406 +  };                                                          
      407 +                                                              
      408 +  return withTransaction(db, run);                            
      409 +}                                                             
      410 +                                                              
      411 +/**                                                           
      412 + * Releases whatever a reservation still holds — making those 
          +coins immediately visible to every                            
      413 + * other concurrent operation on the wallet. Idempotent: calli
          +ng it again on an already-resolved                            
      414 + * reservation is a safe no-op. Marked SETTLED if the reservat
          +ion had nothing left to free (fully                           
      415 + * consumed by settlement already) or RELEASED if it's giving 
          +back an unused remainder.                                     
      416 + */                                                           
      417 +export async function releaseReservation(reservationId: string
          +, db: Db = prisma): Promise<WalletReservation> {              
      418 +  const run = async (tx: Prisma.TransactionClient) => {       
      419 +    const reservation = await tx.walletReservation.findUniqueO
          +rThrow({ where: { id: reservationId } });

          +ion.amountPaise;

          +aise = ${newReserved}, updated_at = now() WHERE id = ${wallet.
          +id}`;

      427 +      data: { amountPaise: 0, status: reservation.amountPaise 
          +> 0 ? "RELEASED" : "SETTLED", resolvedAt: new Date() },       
      428 +    });                                                       
      429 +  };                                                          
      430 +                                                              
      431 +  return withTransaction(db, run);                            
      432 +}

● Checkpoint 2 complete — verified against Docker Postgres

  Schema added (new migration
  20261110150000_add_wallet_reservations_and_tool_billing, not yet applied to 
  Neon — awaiting your go-ahead):
  - WalletReservation + WalletReservationPurpose/WalletReservationStatus enums —
  the database-backed hold mechanism.
  - ToolPrice (new prisma/schema/toolBilling.prisma) — isActive defaults false,
  enforced at the schema level.
  - ToolExecution + ToolExecutionStatus enum — not wired to any route yet
  (that's a later checkpoint); the shape exists so the executionId/resultPayload
  idempotency design from the plan is ready.

  Service layer (wallet.service.ts): added createReservation (graceful
  degradation to whatever's available, never a hard rejection),
  topUpReservation, settleFromReservation (draws from the reservation first,
  covers shortfall straight from the wallet, reports the exact exhaustion
  boundary), releaseReservation (idempotent). Removed the now-dead legacy
  debit() wrapper since nothing calls it anymore.

  Wired into existing chat billing (chat.service.ts, billing.ts) — deliberately
  scoped to only the reservation mechanics, not the rounding formula (still
  Math.round per-tick — that fix stays in Checkpoint 4 as planned) or the state
  machine (still ACTIVE/ENDED only — REQUESTED/PAUSED stay in Checkpoint 3):
  - startSession now reserves a 30s lookahead window up front.
  - Each tick settles from that reservation (falling back to direct wallet draw
  if the reservation's exhausted) and tops it back up.
  - endSession releases whatever's left unused.

  Verified for real in Docker (fresh container, pushed the exact
  pre-Checkpoint-2 baseline, applied the hand-written migration SQL on top,
  regenerated the client): all 5 reservation-primitive tests pass, plus a full
  chat-integration test (startSession → correctly-sized reservation → endSession
  → correct release) — caught and fixed one fixture bug along the way (test
  balance was below MIN_BILLABLE_SECONDS's requirement) and one pool-size
  artifact (unrelated to the code under test). Re-ran Checkpoint 1's suite
  alongside it — no regression.

  Container torn down; real Neon DB reconfirmed untouched (15 users, unchanged).

  20261110150000_add_wallet_reservations_and_tool_billing now (npx prisma

          + number }>();                                                 
       65 +                                                              
       66 +export function recordHeartbeat(sessionId: string, role: ChatS
          +enderRole): void {

       68 +  if (role === "USER") entry.user = Date.now();               
       69 +  else entry.astrologer = Date.now();

       74 +function isPresent(sessionId: string, role: ChatSenderRole): b

          +IMEOUT_MS;

       84 +async function pauseSession(sessionId: string, now: Date): Pro
          +mise<void> {

          +es at all while paused, not merely a tick that computes zero  
       86 +  const presenceGraceEndsAt = new Date(now.getTime() + RECONNE
          +CT_GRACE_MS);                                                 
       87 +  const updated = await prisma.chatSession.updateMany({       
       88 +    where: { id: sessionId, status: "ACTIVE" }, // no-op if it

       89 +    data: { status: "PAUSED", pausedAt: now, presenceGraceEnds
          +At },                                                         
       90 +  });

          +onId, graceEndsAt: presenceGraceEndsAt });

       95 +/** Called whenever a heartbeat arrives for a PAUSED session —
          + resumes only once BOTH sides are present again. */           
       96 +async function maybeResumeFromPause(sessionId: string): Promis
          +e<void> {                                                     
       97 +  const session = await prisma.chatSession.findUnique({ where:
          + { id: sessionId } });                                        
       98 +  if (!session || session.status !== "PAUSED") return;        
       99 +  if (!bothPresent(sessionId)) return;                        
      100 +                                                              
      101 +  const now = new Date();                                     
      102 +  // lastBilledAt is reset to the resume moment, not the pause
          + moment — the paused interval is                              
      103 +  // categorically excluded from billing, never retroactively 
          +charged.                                                      
      104 +  const updated = await prisma.chatSession.updateMany({       
      105 +    where: { id: sessionId, status: "PAUSED" },               
      106 +    data: { status: "ACTIVE", lastBilledAt: now, pausedAt: nul
          +l, presenceGraceEndsAt: null },                               
      107 +  });

      232 +/**                                                           
      233 + * Durable expiry sweep — every deadline it checks (requestExp
          +iresAt, presenceGraceEndsAt) is                               
      234 + * read fresh from the database, never from an in-memory timer
          +, so this is the actual source of

          +ce window. Runs on an interval

          +ght the moment it comes back up.

      239 +export async function sweepExpiredState(): Promise<void> {    
      240 +  const now = new Date();                                     
      241 +                                                              
      242 +  const staleRequests = await prisma.chatSession.findMany({   
      243 +    where: { status: "REQUESTED", requestExpiresAt: { lte: now
          + } },                                                         
      244 +  });                                                         
      245 +  for (const s of staleRequests) {                            
      246 +    await endSession(s.id, "TIMEOUT");                        
      247 +  }                                                           
      248 +                                                              
      249 +  const expiredPauses = await prisma.chatSession.findMany({   
      250 +    where: { status: "PAUSED", presenceGraceEndsAt: { lte: now
          + } },                                                         
      251 +  });                                                         
      252 +  for (const s of expiredPauses) {                            
      253 +    // Billable time up to the pause is already committed (bil
          +ledSeconds/amountPaise/lastBilledAt                           
      254 +    // were frozen at the moment of pausing) — ending here add
          +s nothing further for the gap.                                
      255 +    await endSession(s.id, "DISCONNECTED");                   
      256 +  }                                                           
      257 +}                                                             
      258 +                                                              
      259 +function ensureSweepRunning(): void {                         
      260 +  if (sweepTimer) return;                                     
      261 +  sweepTimer = setInterval(() => {                            
      262 +    sweepExpiredState().catch((err) => console.error("[chat.bi
          +lling] sweepExpiredState failed:", err));                     
      263 +  }, SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS);                                      
      264 +}                                                             
      265 +                                                              
      266 +/**                                                           
      267 + * Called once at server startup. Every session the database t
          +hinks is ACTIVE survived an                                   
      268 + * unknown amount of downtime — treated exactly like a disconn
          +ect (paused, not blindly resumed),                            
      269 + * because there is no way to know whether anyone was actually
          + present during the outage.                                   
      270 + * Billing never resumes from `lastBilledAt` blindly; it resum
          +es only once fresh presence                                   
      271 + * (post-restart heartbeats) re-establishes both sides, via th
          +e same `maybeResumeFromPause` path                            
      272 + * a normal reconnect uses.

● Now restore Checkpoint 3's chat.prisma and apply the two migrations

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Postgres enforces this restriction even across statements within one file —
  the SET DEFAULT 'REQUESTED' later in migration 1 already counts as "using" the
  new enum value in the same transaction. Splitting more precisely: migration 1
  becomes enum-labels-only, migration 2 absorbs everything else (columns,
  backfill, default, indexes).

● Checkpoint 3 complete — verified against Docker Postgres (20/20 tests passing)

  This was the largest behavioral change in the plan. Summary:

  Schema (two sequential migrations — Postgres requires the enum-add to commit
  before anything can reference the new labels, confirmed experimentally when my
  first draft tried to combine them):
  - 20261111100000_add_chat_state_machine — adds REQUESTED/PAUSED to
  ChatSessionStatus, REJECTED/DISCONNECTED to ChatEndReason. Nothing else (had
  to split further than originally planned once Postgres rejected even the SET 
  DEFAULT in the same transaction).
  - 20261111100100_add_chat_session_uniqueness — new ChatSession columns
  (requestedAt, requestExpiresAt, acceptedAt, pausedAt, presenceGraceEndsAt),
  backfill, and the two partial unique indexes
  (chat_sessions_one_active_per_user/...per_astrologer) enforcing one
  REQUESTED/ACTIVE/PAUSED session per user and per astrologer — the exact same
  pattern already established in this repo's astrologer_partial_indexes.sql.

  Behavior changes:
  - startSession now creates REQUESTED, claims nothing (no free-trial slot, no
  wallet reservation) — those only happen in the new acceptSession. A rejected
  or expired request costs the user nothing.
  - New acceptSession/rejectSession + POST /sessions/:id/accept and /reject
  astrologer endpoints. startSession now catches the DB's P2002 unique-violation
  and returns a clean 409 instead of relying on a race-prone findFirst.
  - Presence: chat:heartbeat socket event (plus chat:join/chat:message count as
  heartbeats), tracked in-memory; a tick with no presence from either side
  pauses the session (PAUSED, pausedAt, presenceGraceEndsAt persisted) instead
  of billing through it.
  - A durable periodic sweep (sweepExpiredState, every 10s, reading only DB
  columns — never in-memory timers) ends stale REQUESTED rows as TIMEOUT and
  expired PAUSED rows as DISCONNECTED.
  - Backend restart: every ACTIVE session is treated as a disconnect (paused
  immediately) rather than resuming billing blindly — it only reactivates once
  fresh post-restart heartbeats arrive.

  Caught by the tests, not by inspection: two of my own test fixtures were
  leaving astrologers "busy" forever (never ending their sessions), which the
  uniqueness constraint correctly rejected on later tests — i.e., the fixture
  bugs were the DB constraint working as designed, not a flaw in it.

  Not yet applied to Neon — both migrations are written and fully verified in
  Docker, but real Neon chat session data is untouched. Given this checkpoint's
  size, I'd like your explicit go-ahead before applying it (same as before: npx 
  prisma migrate deploy, two migrations, no destructive prompts expected since
  both are additive).

  Apply now, or review first?

● Here are the two migrations in full, plus a summary of the logic changes, for
  your review before anything touches Neon.

● Both migrations are additive-only and match the audited live schema (confirmed
  again via introspection, same as Checkpoint 1/2). Against your real data
  specifically: all 3 existing chat_sessions rows are already ENDED, so
  migration 2's status default change and the backfill only affect those 3
  historical rows cosmetically (requested_at backfilled from started_at) —
  nothing changes their actual state, and the uniqueness indexes have zero rows
  to conflict with today.

  Logic changes to review (all in backend/src/modules/chat/ and
  backend/src/realtime/socketServer.ts):

  File: chat.service.ts
  What changed: startSession now creates REQUESTED (not ACTIVE), claims nothing
  yet. New acceptSession/rejectSession. The old duplicate-session findFirst
  check is now just a UX fast-path — the real enforcement is catching
  Postgres's P2002 on insert and mapping it to a 409.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: billing.ts
  What changed: New presence map (recordHeartbeat/isPresent, in-memory only),
  pauseSession/maybeResumeFromPause, sweepExpiredState (reads only DB columns,
  never in-memory timers), resumeAllActiveSessions rewritten to
  pause-not-resume every ACTIVE session on startup. tick() gets one new guard
  at the top: if either side isn't present, pause instead of bill.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: chat.astrologer.route.ts
  What changed: Two new routes: POST /sessions/:id/accept, POST 
  /sessions/:id/reject.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: socketServer.ts
  What changed: New chat:heartbeat event; chat:join and chat:message also count
  as a heartbeat.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: chat.constants.ts
  What changed: New constants: REQUEST_ACCEPT_TIMEOUT_MS (60s),
  HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS (10s), PRESENCE_TIMEOUT_MS (15s), RECONNECT_GRACE_MS
  (60s), SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS (10s) — all the decided values from the plan.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: chat.prisma
  What changed: ChatSessionStatus gains REQUESTED/PAUSED; ChatEndReason gains
  REJECTED/DISCONNECTED; ChatSession gains requestedAt, requestExpiresAt,
  acceptedAt, pausedAt, presenceGraceEndsAt.

  One thing worth your explicit attention: no frontend consumes 
  chat:accepted/chat:paused/chat:resumed yet — the existing chat UI will
  currently show a chat as pending indefinitely with no visual feedback until
  it's either accepted (and the existing chat:join/message flow takes over
  normally) or it times out. That UI work isn't part of this backend checkpoint;
  flagging it so it's a known gap, not a surprise, if you test end-to-end
  before frontend work catches up.

  Take your time — let me know if anything needs changing, or say go-ahead to
  apply the two migrations to Neon.

● Checkpoint 4 complete — verified against Docker Postgres (28/28 tests across 5
  suites)

  Schema (20261112090000_add_free_allowance_and_complimentary, not yet applied
  to Neon):
  - ChatFreeAllowance — the global 60s allowance, lazily created exactly like
  Wallet.
  - ChatSession.freeSecondsUsed, ChatSessionType.COMPLIMENTARY.
  - AstrologerProfile.isComplimentary / complimentaryMaxSeconds.

  Logic:
  - Corrected billing formula (billing.ts's cumulativeChargeDue, exported and
  pure) — replaced the old per-tick Math.round with ceil applied to the
  cumulative billable total, newCharge = chargeDue - previouslySettled. Verified
  both as a pure function and wired end-to-end through real ticks against all
  five finalized examples (1s→₹0.12 ... 60s→₹7.00).
  - Global free allowance (freeAllowance.service.ts) — 60s once, consumed before
  any paid amount, proven to carry over between two different astrologers in
  the same test.
  - Exhaustion now ends at the exact affordable second boundary
  (floor(amountPaise*60/rate)), not the full tick's elapsed time.
  - Complimentary astrologers — unmetered up to a cap (per-astrologer override
  or platform default), zero wallet/reservation/allowance interaction, verified.
  - Old per-astrologer 7-minute trial mechanism removed from the
  creation/eligibility path; ChatFreeTrialUsage table preserved untouched as
  history (Option A, as decided).

  Two real concurrency bugs found and fixed (not from inspection — only surfaced
  because the Docker tests exercise genuine concurrent racing):
  getOrCreateFreeAllowance and getOrCreateWallet both relied on prisma.upsert()
  alone, which isn't safe against two concurrent first-time creates for the same
  brand-new user — the loser surfaced a raw P2002 instead of a clean result.
  Fixed both with the same catch-and-reread pattern already established for the
  Checkpoint 1 signup-bonus race.

  Also caught by the migration process itself, not the code: my checkpoint-4
  baseline setup initially used db push, which silently omits all the
  hand-written CHECK constraints and partial unique indexes from every prior
  checkpoint (those only exist in raw migration SQL, never derivable from
  schema.prisma). Rebuilt the verification properly — a clean pre-Checkpoint-1
  baseline via db push, then all 5 of my migrations applied raw in sequence —
  which is what actually caught the real upsert bugs above. I'll use this proper
  method for all remaining checkpoints.

  Container torn down, real Neon DB reconfirmed untouched (15 users).

  Not yet applied to Neon. Apply now, or review first?
